# Iran Update Evening Special Report, March 7, 2026

*Source: <https://understandingwar.org/research/middle-east/iran-update-evening-special-report-march-7-2026/>*

## Key Takeaways

1. Senior Iranian hardline officials are upset over Iranian President Masoud Pezeshkian’s March 7 statement about Iranian attacks on regional countries. Pezeshkian’s comments reiterated a long-standing regime policy rather than signal a policy shift, yet still drew criticism from hardliners, which highlights the extent of divisions within Iran’s leadership.
2. The combined force expanded its airstrike campaign to include Iranian oil production and storage facilities for the first time on March 7. The IDF struck two oil refineries and two oil storage facilities in Iran. Disruptions to Iran’s energy sector will likely worsen the country’s ongoing energy crisis and lead to more widespread and frequent electricity shortages and outages.
3. An Israeli journalist reported on March 7 that Israel estimates that Iran has about 120 missile launchers remaining. US Central Command (CENTCOM) Commander Admiral Brad Cooper stated on March 5 that ballistic missile attacks from Iran have declined by roughly 90 percent since the strikes began.
4. Ukrainian President Volodymyr Zelensky discussed the Iranian drone threat with Saudi Crown Prince Mohammad bin Salman al Saud on March 7. Iranian drone strikes targeting Saudi territory have damaged civilian infrastructure and forced the closure of Saudi Arabia’s largest domestic oil refinery and export terminal. Zelensky said that Ukraine is “ready to help” Saudi Arabia address the threat posed by Shahed drones.
5. The combined force struck the IRGC Aerospace Force infrastructure in Tehran on March 7, likely to degrade Iranian air defense capabilities and command and control. The IRGC Aerospace Force is the principal operator of the Iranian missile and drone arsenals.

## Toplines

**Senior Iranian hardline officials are upset over Iranian President Masoud Pezeshkian’s March 7 statement about Iranian attacks on regional countries. Pezeshkian’s comments reiterated a long-standing regime policy rather than signal a policy shift, yet still drew criticism from hardliners, which highlights the extent of divisions within Iran’s leadership.**[1]Pezeshkian apologized on March 7 to regional countries that Iran has attacked since the start of the conflict and said that the Leadership Council, which assumed the duties of the supreme leader after his death, ordered the Armed Forces not to attack any country unless that country attacks Iran.[2] Pezeshkian clarified in a post on X that Iranian attacks on regional countries exclusively target US facilities rather than host countries themselves.[3] This claim is false. Iran has struck sites in Middle Eastern countries that are not US bases, including a drone strike on the Hayat Palace Hotel in Bahrain on March 3.[4] Pezeshkian’s statement echoes the regime’s long-standing position on regional attacks. The regime has historically threatened or used military force to pressure Gulf countries to sever ties with the United States and to deter US action against Iran.[5] Iran and Iran-backed actors have previously cited the presence of US assets in Middle Eastern countries to justify indiscriminate attacks on those countries.[6] Hardline parliamentarian Hamid Rasaei dismissed Pezeshkian’s statement as “weak, unprofessional, and unacceptable” and urged the Assembly of Experts to select a new supreme leader soon so it can dissolve the Leadership Council.[7] An unspecified hardliner close to the office of former Supreme Leader Ali Khamenei told Reuters that several senior commanders in the Islamic Revolutionary Guard Corps (IRGC) were also upset with Pezeshkian’s statement.[8] Pezeshkian’s office released a readout of the speech after the backlash that once again reiterated the regime’s position but notably omitted Pezeshkian’s apology to regional countries, which was almost certainly a response to the criticism.[9]

**The combined force expanded its airstrike campaign to include Iranian oil production and storage facilities for the first time on March 7.** The IDF struck two oil refineries and two oil storage facilities in Iran on March 7.[10] This included the Tondgouyan Oil Refinery in Shahr Rey, Tehran Province, and the Shahran Oil Refinery in Tehran City.[11] The Tondgouyan Oil Refinery is one of Iran’s largest oil refineries, and the IDF previously struck it during the 12-Day War.[12] The Shahran Oil Refinery previously had the capacity to store three days’ worth of fuel for Tehran across at least 11 storage tanks.[13] The IDF also struck this facility during the 12-Day War.[14] Israeli forces also struck oil storage facilities in Karaj, Alborz Province, and a separate facility in Tehran City on March 7, marking the first recorded strikes on these facilities.[15] The IDF said that it struck oil production and storage facilities that directly supported Iran’s military industrial complex.[16] Disruptions to Iran’s energy sector will likely worsen the country’s ongoing energy crisis and lead to more widespread and frequent electricity shortages and outages. Iranians have reported daily blackouts due to the energy crisis since February 2025.[17]

[![](https://understandingwar.org/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/Israeli-Strikes-on-Iranian-Oil-Infrastructure-Evening-of-March-7-2026-1-819x1024.webp)](https://understandingwar.org/map/israeli-strikes-on-iranian-oil-infrastructure-from-march-6-2026-at-300-pm-et-to-march-7-2026-at-300-pm-et/)

**An Israeli journalist reported on March 7 that Israel estimates that Iran has about 120 missile launchers remaining.[18]** The journalist’s report is consistent with the IDF’s estimate on March 6 that  Iran has around 100 to 200 missile launchers remaining.[19] US Central Command (CENTCOM) Commander Admiral Brad Cooper stated on March 5 that ballistic missile attacks from Iran have declined by roughly 90 percent since the strikes began.[20]

**Ukrainian President Volodymyr Zelensky discussed the Iranian drone threat with Saudi Crown Prince Mohammad bin Salman al Saud on March 7.[21]** Iranian drone strikes targeting Saudi territory have damaged civilian infrastructure and forced the closure of Saudi Arabia’s largest domestic oil refinery and export terminal.[22] Zelensky said that Ukraine is “ready to help” Saudi Arabia address the threat posed by Shahed drones.[23]  Ukraine has years of experience defending against almost nightly large-scale Russian drone and missile strike packages, which have included Iranian-origin strike drones much like those the United States and its allies currently face in the Middle East.[24] Ukraine has a reported 90 percent interception rate of such drones.[25] Reuters reported on March 5, citing a source familiar with the matter, that US and Qatari officials are in talks with Ukraine to purchase Ukrainian interceptor drones to intercept Iranian Shahed drones.[26]

**USS** ***George H. W. Bush*** **Carrier Strike Group is reportedly expected to deploy soon, potentially to the Middle East.[27]** The US Navy announced that USS *George H. W. Bush* Carrier Strike Group (CSG) completed Composite Training Unit Exercise on March 6.[28] CSG Commander Rear Admiral Alexis Walker said that the completion of the training exercise means that the CSG is “ready for major combat operations around the world.”[29] The US Naval Institute News said that the USS *George H W Bush* CSG is the “next expected [CSG] to deploy from the United States.”[30] The United States currently has two CSGs within the US Central Command area of responsibility in the Red Sea and Persian Gulf.[31]

[![](https://understandingwar.org/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/US-and-Israeli-Strikes-in-Iran-from-March-6-2026-3-PM-ET-to-March-7-2026-3-PM-ET-819x1024.webp)](https://understandingwar.org/map/us-and-israeli-strikes-in-iran-from-march-6-2026-300-pm-et-to-march-7-2026-300-pm-et/)

## US and Israeli Air Campaign

**The combined US-Israeli force struck Iranian military assets and headquarters, internal security forces, defense industrial sites, and oil infrastructure across western, southwestern, and central Iran on March 7.** US Central Command (CENTCOM) reported on March 6 that it has struck over 3,000 targets in Iran since the beginning of the campaign on February 28.[32] The IDF announced on March 7 that it struck over 300 targets in Iran over the past two days.[33]

**The IDF struck the Islamic Revolutionary Guards Corps (IRGC) General Command headquarters and other internal security forces in southeastern Tehran on March 4.[34]** Commercially available satellite imagery captured on March 6 showed damage to several IRGC-affiliated headquarters, compounds, storage facilities, and possible training facilities. The imagery showed extensive damage to four buildings within the IRGC General Command Headquarters.[35] The IRGC is the primary body responsible for controlling the Iranian missile and drone arsenals and managing and supporting the so-called “Axis of Resistance.”[36]

The IRGC General Command Headquarters presumably also oversees IRGC units that function primarily as an internal security force. The 27th Mohammad Rasoul Ollah Operational Division, for example, is the IRGC Ground Forces’ provincial unit for Tehran City.[37] The IDF also struck the Mohammad Rasoul Ollah Operational Division on March 4.[38] Satellite imagery from March 6 showed extensive damage to at least six buildings or structures on the operational division’s compound. The IDF also struck a facility about three kilometers west of the Mohammad Rasoul Ollah Operational Division that the IDF identified in a graphic as the headquarters for the ”Internal Security unit responsible for suppressing protests.[39] The IDF also assigned an IRGC logo to the facility in the graphic, which suggests that it is an IRGC-affiliated entity with an internal security mission. At least several of the buildings in the compound appear to have housed IRGC military police as of 2012.[40]

![](https://understandingwar.org/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/East-of-IRGC-HQ-Afternoon-of-March-7-2026-1.gif "East of IRGC HQ, Afternoon of March 7, 2026 (1)")

**The combined force struck the IRGC Aerospace Force infrastructure in Tehran on March 7, likely to degrade Iranian air defense capabilities and command and control.** The IRGC Aerospace Force is the principal operator of the Iranian missile and drone arsenals.[41] The IDF struck the Aerospace Force air defense command center west of Tehran on March 7.[42] The Aerospace Force has some air defense platforms and aircraft that it uses in collaboration with the Artesh Air Defense Force to defend Iranian airspace.[43] The IDF reported that it also hit air defense systems, headquarters, logistical warehouses, and other buildings surrounding the air defense center.[44] Iranian sources posted footage of a purported airstrike on the Aerospace Force’s headquarters in Kuhak neighborhood, Tehran, on March 7.[45] The facility in Kuhak is the Aerospace Force’s service headquarters.

**The combined force has continued to target Iranian ballistic missile manufacturing sites as part of an effort to degrade Iran’s ballistic missile program.** Iranian media posted footage of explosions at Parchin Military Complex on March 5, and the IDF announced on March 7 that it had struck a ballistic missile production site at the complex.[46] It is unclear if the combined force conducted two separate strikes on March 5 and then a later date, or if the IDF was confirming the March 5 strike on March 7. Iran has used the Parchin complex to develop and manufacture advanced munitions, including drones and missiles.[47] The Institute for Science and International Security assessed that airstrikes targeting Parchin on March 5 destroyed two to three solid propellant production facilities.[48] The IDF previously destroyed these facilities in October 2024 and in June 2025, but Iran subsequently acquired new equipment.[49] The combined force has also struck Parchin’s underground facility.[50]

The IDF also announced on March 7 that it struck the IRGC Shahroud Missile Facility in Semnan Province within the past week.[51] The IRGC uses the facility to develop, test, and launch solid-propellant missiles.[52] The IDF identified two complexes at the facility for mixing and casting missile engines and warheads, in addition to a launch area.[53] The IDF previously struck the facility during its October 2024 airstrikes on Iran and during the June 2025 12-Day War.[54] The IDF targeted planetary mixers and missile engine manufacturing equipment during the June strikes.[55]

The combined force struck at least two buildings at the Khojir Missile Production Complex in Hajarabad, Tehran Province, likely on March 3.[56] The Khojir complex is a research, development, and manufacturing facility for solid fuel missiles.[57] An analyst from the James Martin Center for Non-Proliferation Studies assessed that the strikes damaged several buildings that host planetary mixers and casting facilities.[58] The Iranian regime expanded the Khojir complex in August 2023 in order to increase missile production capacity.[59]

**The combined force continued to strike Iran’s defense industrial base to degrade its ballistic missile, air defense, nuclear, and drone programs.** Satellite imagery published on March 6 showed that the combined force struck the Shiraz Electronics Industries (SEI) Zone in Fars Province at least 13 times.[60] SEI produces military electronics for the Iranian armed forces, including radars, avionics, and missile guidance components, and is under US sanctions.[61] Repeated combined forces strikes on SEI, including a previous strike on March 3, likely aim to disrupt production that supports Iran’s air defense and drone industries.[62]

Satellite imagery from March 6 also showed strikes on the Raja Shimi Industries plant in Tehran Province, located adjacent to the Imam Sajjad Missile Base.[63] The facility is likely involved in Iranian missile production, given its proximity to the missile base and a report that it produced rocket propellants.[64] The combined force previously struck the site on March 4, and the IDF reported three unexplained explosions at the facility in 2015, which further suggests the facility’s involvement in missile production.[65]

An Iranian OSINT account reported that the combined force likely struck the Esteghlal Industrial Zone in Tehran Province again, after previously targeting the facility on March 6.[66] The industrial zone includes companies tied to Iran’s defense industry, including Azmoon Pajohan Hesgar LLC, which the United States sanctioned in 2024 for links to Iran’s Ministry of Defense and Armed Forces Logistics (MODAFL).[67] The IDF previously issued an evacuation order for the Esteghlal Industrial Zone on March 3.[68]

Geolocated video from March 6 also indicates that the combined force struck the Defense Industries Organization (DIO) facility in Tehran at least three times.[69] The DIO, a subsidiary of Iran’s Ministry of Defense and Armed Forces Logistics (MODAFL), oversees key segments of Iran’s military industrial base and supports the country’s missile and nuclear programs.[70] The United States, the United Nations, and several Western countries have sanctioned the DIO.[71] The combined force likely struck the facility previously on March 2.[72]

An Iranian OSINT account also reported that combined force airstrikes may have targeted ballistic missile launchers at the Jahan Electric Industrial Park in Meybod, Yazd Province, on March 7.[73] The Jahan Electric Industrial Park procured carbon fiber for Iran’s nuclear program, which Iran used to manufacture advanced centrifuge rotors and ballistic missile components.[74] The Jahan Electric Industrial Park is sanctioned by the United States.[75]

**The IDF destroyed IRGC Quds Force-affiliated aircraft and several facilities at Mehrabad Airport on March 6 that allegedly facilitated Iranian weapons transfers to the Axis of Resistance, including Lebanese Hezbollah.[76]** The IDF struck Mehrabad Airport on March 6, according to the IDF and Iranian media.[77] Commercially available satellite imagery captured on March 7 showed extensive damage to buildings and support facilities at the airport. The imagery also showed that strikes destroyed at least seven cargo aircraft. The IDF stated that it struck 16 Quds Force-affiliated aircraft that had transported weapons to Hezbollah in Lebanon in the past.[78] These statements could refer to aircraft owned and operated by Mahan Air. Mahan Air is a US-sanctioned Iranian airline that has frequently transported Quds Force funds, weapons, and operatives in support of Iranian proxy activities abroad and to support Hezbollah directly.[79] Lebanese airport security forces searched an Iranian Mahan Air flight in Beirut on suspicion of transporting funds to Hezbollah in January 2025, for example.[80]

The IDF also struck a number of Iranian fighter jets at Mehrabad Airport.[81] The Artesh Air Force 1st Tactical Airbase is co-located at Mehrabad Airport.[82]

![Mehrabad International Airport, March 7, 2026](https://understandingwar.org/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/Mehrabad-International-Airport-2-Afternoon-of-March-7-2026.gif "Mehrabad International Airport, March 7, 2026")![Mehrabad International Airport, March 7, 2026](https://understandingwar.org/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/Mehrabad-International-Airport-1-Afternoon-of-March-7-2026.gif "Mehrabad International Airport, March 7, 2026")

**The US-Israeli combined force continued to target internal security infrastructure across Iran, likely to degrade the regime’s ability to maintain domestic control and counter militant activity.** Satellite imagery from March 6 showed extensive damage to a base in Tehran City that the IDF identified as the IRGC Intelligence Organization’s headquarters. The imagery shows several destroyed buildings in the western and southeastern sections of the compound. The IRGC created the Intelligence Organization in response to the Green Movement protests in 2009.[83] The Intelligence Organization has thus focused primarily on domestic surveillance, social control, and counterinsurgency operations, while also conducting limited operations abroad. The Intelligence Organization’s headquarters is close to the Imam Hassan Security Unit and the 13th Tehran Municipality Region’s Komeil Basij Resistance Regional Base.

![](https://understandingwar.org/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/Imam-Hassan-Security-Unit-Afternoon-of-March-7-2026-1.gif "Imam Hassan Security Unit, Afternoon of March 7, 2026 (1)")

## Iranian Retaliation

**Iran launched ten missile attack waves at Israel between March 6 at 3:00 PM and March 7 at 3:00 PM.[95]** Two Iranian missiles struck open areas in Israel during this period, one in northern Israel and one in central Israel.[96] Iran has launched at least six missile waves per day at Israel since March 2.[97] The IDF announced on March 7 that Israel had intercepted over 110 Iranian drones since the start of the conflict.[98]

[![Iranian and Axis Retaliatory Strikes in the Middle East Between March 6, 2026 at 3:00 PM ET and March 7, 2026 at 3:00 PM ET](https://understandingwar.org/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/Iranian-Retalitory-Strikes-Evening-of-March-7-2026-819x1024.webp)](https://understandingwar.org/map/iranian-and-axis-retaliatory-strikes-in-the-middle-east-between-march-6-2026-at-300-pm-et-and-march-7-2026-at-300-pm-et/)

**Iran continued to attack regional countries on March 7 after Iranian President Masoud Pezeshkian’s order to suspend strikes on Gulf states unless attacks originated from their countries.** The Saudi Arabian Defense Ministry announced that an Iranian ballistic missile fell in an uninhabited area after Iran attempted to attack the Prince Sultan Airbase.[99] Unidentified Saudi officials also told the Wall Street Journal that a likely Iranian drone targeted the Saudi Aramco Berri oil field.[100] CTP-ISW assessed on March 2 that Iran is likely targeting energy infrastructure in Gulf countries to compel the United States and its partners to pursue a ceasefire before achieving their objective of collapsing the regime.[101] Saudi Arabia also intercepted one other Iranian ballistic missile attack, as well as eight drones, since March 6 at 3:00 PM ET.[102] Iran also continued to attack the United Arab Emirates (UAE), which has faced the most Iranian attacks since February 28.[103] The Emirati Defense Ministry announced that Iran has launched 121 drones and 16 ballistic missiles at the UAE since 3:00 PM on March 6.[104] Qatar also intercepted an Iranian ballistic missile attack on March 7.[105]

![](https://understandingwar.org/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/Iranian-Launches-at-the-UAE-March-7-1024x768.webp)

**Iranian-backed Iraqi militias continue to conduct attacks and claim attacks targeting US forces, including the first militia attack on the US Embassy in Baghdad since 2023.** US air defenses intercepted a likely militia rocket attack targeting the US Embassy in Baghdad on March 7.[106] An OSINT account reported that the rockets were fired from eastern Baghdad.[107] CTP-ISW had assessed prior to the start of the conflict that Iranian-backed Iraqi militias would likely prioritize attacking US forces in Iraqi Kurdistan over the US Embassy in Baghdad due to the negative ramifications such an attack would likely have on militia financial holdings in Iraq by deterring investment in the country.[108] Iranian-backed Iraqi militias last attacked the US Embassy in Baghdad in 2023.[109] The Islamic Resistance in Iraq, which is a coalition of Iranian-backed Iraqi militias, claimed on March 6 that it had conducted 23 drone and missile operations targeting US and enemy bases in the region in the past 24 hours.[110] CTP-ISW has not yet observed any indications of Iraqi militias using missiles in their attacks as of this writing. Geolocated footage posted on X showed smoke rising from a hotel in Erbil after a drone attack on March 6.[111] Likely Iranian-backed Iraqi militia front group Rijal al Baas al Shadid claimed that it targeted US forces with unidentified projectiles at a hotel in Erbil on March 6.[112] CTP-ISW assessed on March 6 that Iranian-backed Iraqi militias may be using front groups in an attempt to avoid US and Israeli strikes on militia positions and obfuscate responsibility.[113]

**The United States and Israel continue to conduct attacks targeting Iranian-backed Iraqi militia positions in Iraq in order to deter the militias from retaliating against the United States and Israel.** A security source told Iraqi media on February 7 that aircraft struck unspecified sites in Jurf al Sakhr, which is a Kataib Hezbollah stronghold south of Baghdad.[114] The combined force has repeatedly struck Kataib Hezbollah positions in Jurf al Sakhr in recent days.[115] The combined force reportedly struck a Kataib al Imam Ali headquarters south of Mosul on March 7.[116] The combined force also struck Kataib Hezbollah and Iranian-backed Popular Mobilization Forces (PMF) positions in southeastern Anbar Province, according to Iraqi media.[117] Kataib Hezbollah and other Iranian-backed Iraqi militias control brigades within the PMF that answer to Iran instead of the Iraqi Prime Minister.[118] An airstrike targeting a PMF 33rd Brigade headquarters south of Mosul, which caused minor injuries and material damage.[119] The 33rd PMF Brigade is affiliated with the armed wing of an Iraqi party that broke away from Iraqi Shia nationalist cleric Muqtada al Sadr.[120]

[[![Map Thumbnail](https://understandingwar.org/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/US-and-Israeli-Strikes-on-Iraq-Afternoon-of-March-7-2026.webp)](https://understandingwar.org/map/us-and-israeli-strikes-on-iraq-between-march-6-2026-at-300-pm-et-and-march-7-2026-at-300-pm-et/)](https://understandingwar.org/map/us-and-israeli-strikes-on-iraq-between-march-6-2026-at-300-pm-et-and-march-7-2026-at-300-pm-et/ "US and Israeli Strikes on Iraq Between March 6, 2026 at 3:00 PM ET and March 7, 2026 at 3:00 PM ET")

## Supreme Leader Succession

**Assembly of Experts member Hossein Mozafari stated on March 7 that the Assembly of Experts will likely convene within the next 24 hours to deliberate and select the next supreme leader.**[121] Supreme National Security Council Secretary Ali Larijani stated on March 7 that the Leadership Council has ordered security forces to prepare the necessary security measures to allow the Assembly of Experts to meet.[122] There are several unconfirmed reports that the Assembly of Experts will select Khamenei’s son, Mojtaba Khamenei.[123] A Western security official told the *Washington Post* on March 6 that the IRGC has been pushing for Mojtaba but has faced resistance from other senior regime officials, including Larijani.[124]

## Lebanon

**Hezbollah claimed that it conducted 23 attacks targeting the IDF in southern Lebanon and Israeli defense sites, industrial facilities, and cities in northern Israel since CTP-ISW’s data cutoff at 3:00 PM ET on March 6.[125]** Hezbollah claimed that it engaged IDF vehicles with rocket-propelled grenades (RPGs) in Kfar Kila, Marjaayoun District.[126] The IDF reported that two 84th (Givati) Infantry Brigade (162nd Division) soldiers were injured by Hezbollah anti-tank fire in southern Lebanon, though it is unclear if this occurred during this engagement with Hezbollah.[127] Hezbollah also claimed that it launched rockets and drones at IDF positions in Jabal Balat, Tyre District, and Tal al Hamamis, Wadi al Asafir, Markaba, and Khiam, all in Marjaayoun[128].[129] Hezbollah has targeted all these locations repeatedly since the start of the conflict.[130] Two of Israel’s five permanent positions in southern Lebanon that it has occupied since February 2025 are located at Tal al Hamamis and Jabal Balat.[131] Hezbollah also claimed that it launched rockets and drones targeting six IDF bases in northern Israel.[132] Hezbollah previously attacked many of these IDF bases during the October 7 War.[133] Hezbollah also claimed that it targeted multiple industrial sites near Haifa, including an oil refinery and a defense industrial site.[134] Hezbollah targeted two Kiryat Shmona and Nahariya, which are two northern Israeli towns, with rockets and drones.[135] Hezbollah issued statements in Hebrew calling on residents of both cities to evacuate prior to the group’s claimed rocket and drone launches.[136] Israeli media reported on March 7 that one Hezbollah drone crashed in a medical center parking lot and Israeli civil defense shot down another.[137] There were no reported casualties or significant damage following these incidents. Hezbollah is likely continuing to attempt to displace northern Israeli residents from their homes to place political pressure on the Israeli government to curtail its war effort.[138]

[[![Map Thumbnail](https://understandingwar.org/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/Hezbollah-Attacks-in-Northern-Israel-and-Southern-Lebanon-from-March-6-2026-at-3-PM-ET-to-March-7-2026-at-3-PM-ET-scaled.webp)](https://understandingwar.org/map/hezbollah-attacks-in-northern-israel-and-southern-lebanon-from-march-6-2026-at-300-pm-et-to-march-7-2026-at-300-pm-et/)](https://understandingwar.org/map/hezbollah-attacks-in-northern-israel-and-southern-lebanon-from-march-6-2026-at-300-pm-et-to-march-7-2026-at-300-pm-et/ "Hezbollah Attacks in Northern Israel and Southern Lebanon From March 6, 2026 at 3:00 PM ET to March 7, 2026 at 3:00 PM ET")

**The IDF has continued to conduct airstrikes targeting Hezbollah military sites in Lebanon to degrade Hezbollah’s ability to conduct retaliatory attacks against Israel.** The IDF reported on March 7 that it had attacked over 170 targets in Lebanon over the last 48 hours.[139] The IDF said that it continues to target Hezbollah rocket launchers, weapons depots, and personnel in southern Lebanon and the Bekaa Valley.[140] The IDF also struck Radwan Force infrastructure and multiple commanders in Majdal Salam, Marjaayoun District. [141]The IDF issued evacuation orders for all residents south of the Litani River and in Beirut’s southern suburbs.[142] The IDF previously issued evacuation orders for these areas on March 4 and March 5, prior to conducting airstrikes against Hezbollah targets.[143]

IDF special operations forces (SOF) conducted a raid into Nabi Chit, Baalbek District, to search for the remains of missing IAF pilot Ron Arad.[144] Arad was captured by Amal in 1986 after he was shot down over Lebanon.[145] The IDF SOF, acting on new intelligence, flew into the area on four helicopters and investigated a grave site in Nabi Chit.[146] As the IDF unit withdrew, Hezbollah and local residents engaged the Israeli forces with small arms and rocket fire.[147] The IDF reported that the special forces unit suffered no casualties during the mission.[148] The Lebanese Armed Forces (LAF) reported that Israeli strikes killed three LAF soldiers and multiple civilians in the vicinity of Nabi Chit during the Israeli raid.[149] The IDF and Israeli Prime Minister Benjamin Netanyahu said that the raid did not yield any findings related to Arad.[150] Netanyahu said that the raid demonstrated his commitment to finding all Israeli missing persons and prisoners.[151]

[[![Map Thumbnail](https://understandingwar.org/wp-content/uploads/2026/03/Israeli-Strikes-in-Lebanon-from-March-6-2026-at-3-PM-ET-to-March-7-2026-at-3-PM-ET-scaled.webp)](https://understandingwar.org/map/israeli-strikes-in-lebanon-from-march-6-2026-at-300-pm-et-to-march-7-2026-at-300-pm-et/)](https://understandingwar.org/map/israeli-strikes-in-lebanon-from-march-6-2026-at-300-pm-et-to-march-7-2026-at-300-pm-et/ "Israeli Strikes in Lebanon From March 6, 2026 at 3:00 PM ET to March 7, 2026 at 3:00 PM ET")

**Iranian diplomats have reportedly begun to leave Lebanon amid continued Israeli and Lebanese pressure.** A senior Lebanese security source told Reuters that over 150 Iranian nationals, including non-essential diplomats, departed from Lebanon on March 7.[152] The source said another 20 left on March 6.[153] The source said that the Iranians were being flown to Russia on a Russian plane.[154] A source from the Iranian Embassy in Lebanon said that the non-essential Iranian diplomats were leaving due to Israeli threats and strikes near the Iranian Embassy on March 6.[155] IRGC Quds Force members have also reportedly begun to leave Lebanon in recent days.[156] The Lebanese government announced on March 5 that it will ban any IRGC activity in Lebanon and seeks to deport IRGC members from Lebanon.[157] The government also decided that Iranian civilians will no longer have visa-free entry into Lebanon.[158]

**The Lebanese Government is reportedly considering legal action against Hezbollah Secretary General Naim Qassem.[159]** A source close to Lebanese Justice Minister Adel Nassar told Lebanese media on March 7 that Nassar is considering taking legal action against Qassem.[160] The Lebanese Cabinet declared on March 2 that all Hezbollah military and security activity is illegal.[161] No previous Lebanese government has attempted or considered prosecuting a Hezbollah secretary-general.

# Iran Update Evening Special Report, March 8, 2026

*Source: <https://www.criticalthreats.org/analysis/iran-update-evening-special-report-march-8-2026-69ae4679967f0>*

**Data Cutoff: 03:00 PM ET**

*The Critical Threats Project (CTP) at the American Enterprise Institute and the Institute for the Study of War (ISW) are publishing two updates daily to provide analysis on the war with Iran. The morning update will focus on US and Israeli strikes on Iran and the Axis of Resistance’s response to the strikes. The evening update will be more comprehensive, covering events over the past 24-hour period and refining items discussed in the morning update.*

**Key Takeaways:**

- Iran’s Assembly of Experts selected former Supreme Leader Ali Khamenei’s son, Mojtaba Khamenei, as the next Supreme Leader on March 8. Mojtaba’s selection as the Supreme Leader represents a victory for hardline factions over more pragmatic figures within the regime. Mojtaba will face several immediate challenges, including trying to establish his legitimacy and attempting to unite and gain the support of various regime factions. Mojtaba’s connections to the IRGC and the Office of the Supreme Leader may mitigate some of these challenges.
- Ukraine will send an unspecified number of Ukrainian military personnel with expertise in downing Shahed drones to Gulf states, underscoring how Ukraine’s experience in defending against Iranian drones can strengthen the air defenses of the United States and its allies in the Middle East.
- The US-Israeli combined force continued to target Iranian ballistic missile infrastructure in order to degrade Iranian missile capabilities and ultimately destroy the Iranian ballistic missile program. The IDF has attacked over 400 targets, including ballistic missile launchers and weapons production sites, in western and central Iran over the past day. The IDF has destroyed around 75 percent of Iran's missile launchers.
- The combined force continued to target Iranian internal security institutions in Tehran City and western Iran. OSINT accounts reported combined force airstrikes targeting LEC headquarters in Borujerd, Lorestan Province, Kuhdasht, Lorestan Province, Eslamabad-e Gharb, Kermanshah Province, and Abdanan, Ilam Province.
- Iran continued to attack regional countries on March 7 and 8. An Iranian drone damaged a desalination plant in Bahrain. The Saudi Civil Defense Authority reported on March 8 that an unspecified projectile fell on a residential area in Kharj, Saudi Arabia, killing two individuals and wounding 12 others.
- The IDF has continued to conduct airstrikes targeting Hezbollah across Lebanon to degrade Hezbollah’s ability to launch attacks targeting Israeli forces and positions in northern Israel and southern Lebanon. The IDF has conducted over 100 strikes in Lebanon in the past 24 hours and a total of 600 strikes using 820 munitions in Lebanon since the war began on February 28.

**Toplines**

**Iran’s Assembly of Experts selected former Supreme Leader Ali Khamenei’s son, Mojtaba Khamenei, as the next Supreme Leader on March 8.[[i]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef31)** **Mojtaba’s selection as the Supreme Leader represents a victory for hardline factions over more pragmatic** **figures within the regime.** Mojtaba is a hardline cleric who will likely pursue similar domestic and foreign policies to those of his father. Mojtaba fought in the Iran-Iraq War and developed important relationships while serving in the Habib Ibn Mazahir Battalion under the 27th Mohammad Rasoul Allah Division.[[ii]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef32) Many members of the Habib Battalion went on to hold influential positions, particularly in the Islamic Revolutionary Guards Corps (IRGC) Intelligence Organization.[[iii]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef33) Mojtaba maintained ties to these individuals, such as former IRGC Intelligence Organization Chief Hossein Taeb. Mojtaba used these relationships to cement his role in Iran’s security apparatus. Mojtaba reportedly played a leading role in securing former hardline President Mahmoud Ahmadinejad’s victory in 2009 and subsequently took control of the Basij to crush the protests over Ahmadinejad’s election, for example.[[iv]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef34) US President Donald Trump told Axios on March 5 that Mojtaba was an unacceptable candidate to become Iran’s new Supreme Leader.[[v]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef35)

Mojtaba will face several immediate challenges, including trying to establish his legitimacy and attempting to unite and gain the support of various regime factions. Mojtaba’s connections to the IRGC and the Office of the Supreme Leader may mitigate some of these challenges, however. Mojtaba is a mid-level cleric like his father was when he assumed the position, which has historically caused his candidacy to be controversial.[[vi]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef36) Prominent clerics have publicly questioned Mojtaba’s religious credentials, for example.[[vii]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef37) Mojtaba will also likely face criticisms that the Islamic Republic is transforming into a monarchy with hereditary succession, particularly because Ali Khamenei reportedly opposed hereditary succession.[[viii]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef38) Mojtaba will additionally have to contend with a very fractured regime and attempt to unite and gain the support of various factions at a point when the regime is facing unprecedented internal and external pressure. Anti-regime media recently reported that the IRGC pushed for Mojtaba’s selection.[[ix]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef39) It is unclear which specific factions within the IRGC support Mojtaba, but this support may mitigate some of the challenges Mojtaba will face.

Mojtaba’s selection represents a victory for hardliners over more pragmatic figures within the regime. Some Assembly of Experts members, such as hardline cleric Ayatollah Mohammad Mehdi Mir Bagheri, wanted the Assembly of Experts to quickly appoint a new Supreme Leader after Ali Khamenei’s death, likely, in part, to make it difficult for those who opposed Mojtaba’s candidacy to have sufficient time to convince others to oppose Mojtaba.[[x]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef310) More pragmatic figures within the regime appear to oppose Mojtaba. Anti-regime media reported on March 6 that Supreme National Security Council Secretary Ali Larijani had wanted his brother, Sadegh Amoli Larijani, to become the next Supreme Leader, which would have given Ali Larijani immense power within the regime.[[xi]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef311) Anti-regime media previously reported in September 2025 that Ali Larijani was “competing to position [himself] for a potential succession.“[[xii]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef312) The *New York Times* reported on February 22 that Khamenei had tasked Ali Larijani with “steering the country” during the recent protests and that Larijani has "effectively been running the country" since then.[[xiii]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef313)

NOTE: A version of the following text also appears in The Institute for the Study of War's March 8 Russian Offensive Campaign Assessment:

**Ukraine will send an unspecified number of Ukrainian military personnel with expertise in downing Shahed drones to Gulf states, underscoring how Ukraine’s experience in defending against Iranian drones can strengthen the air defenses of the United States and its allies in the Middle East.** Ukrainian President Volodymyr Zelensky stated on March 8 that an unspecified number of Ukrainian experts and military personnel will depart from Ukraine on March 9 to teach unspecified Gulf states Ukraine’s knowledge on how to destroy drones.[[xiv]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef314) Zelensky stated that Ukraine will provide the United States and countries in the Middle East with Ukraine’s expertise and military experience in combating Shahed drones, cruise missiles, and other aerial threats and noted that three unspecified Gulf states seek to buy Ukrainian Shahed interceptors.[[xv]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef315) Zelensky has also spoken with several leaders from Middle Eastern states in recent days, including Bahraini King Hamad bin Isa al Khalifa and Saudi Crown Prince Mohammad bin Salman al Saud, to discuss insights into countering Iranian threats.[[xvi]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef316) Iran has fired thousands of drones targeting Gulf states since February 28, inflicting significant damage on US and Gulf military infrastructure and civilian areas and disrupting regional oil, gas, and international shipping industries.[[xvii]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef317) Ukraine can offer the United States and US allies in the Middle East unique insights into how to combat Iranian strikes because the Ukrainian military has institutionalized and operationalized the fighting experience that Ukraine has learned throughout the past four years of war.

Several Ukrainian companies have the ability to export battle-tested interceptor drones to the United States and US allies. Reuters reported on March 7 that Ukrainian manufacturers of low-cost interceptor drones stated that they have the capacity to export large volumes of drones.[[xviii]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef318) SkyFall, a major Ukrainian drone and drone interceptor manufacturer, estimated on March 7 that it could produce up to 50,000 interceptor drones per month and could export 5,000 to 10,000 drones while still fulfilling Ukraine’s needs. Ukrainian drone manufacturer TAF Industries Head Oleksandr Yakovenko stated on March 8 that the UAE, Qatar, and Kuwait have already requested or expressed interest in importing Ukrainian interceptor drones.[[xix]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef319) Yakovenko noted that it has taken several months to train drone interceptor operators and that training interceptor operators is the main limiting factor for countries looking to use Ukrainian technology. Ukraine’s institutional knowledge has enabled Ukrainian firms to rapidly develop and refine capabilities specifically tailored to counter Iranian-origin weapons, and continued investment into Ukraine’s defense industrial base is important for not just Ukrainian security but also for the United States and its allies.

**US and Israeli Campaign**

**The combined force has damaged runways and taxiways and likely destroyed aircraft at several airbases in central Iran, likely to suppress and destroy Iranian air defenses over the area and extend US and Israeli air dominance into central Iran.** The combined force struck two airbases in and around Esfahan City on March 7.[[xx]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef320) The Israel Defense Forces (IDF) likely struck the 8th Artesh Air Force Tactical Airbase, north of Esfahan City, Esfahan Province, on March 7.[[xxi]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef321) Commercially available satellite imagery captured on March 8 shows about ten impact points, including extensive cratering on the base’s taxiway. The IDF announced on March 8 that it struck structures storing F-14 fighter jets in addition to air defense systems at an unspecified Esfahan airbase, presumably referring to the 8th Tactical Airbase.[[xxii]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef322) The base hosts F-14s, F-7s, and PC-7s.[[xxiii]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef323) The combined force also struck the 4th Artesh Ground Forces Aviation Base in Esfahan City on March 7.[[xxiv]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef324) Commercially available satellite imagery captured on March 8 shows damage to several structures north of the base’s runway, including several large hangars or warehouses. The 4th Artesh Ground Forces Aviation Base hosts rotary-wing aircraft. The combined force also cratered runways and taxiways at the 3rd Artesh Air Force Tactical Airbase in Kabudarahang, Hamedan Province, according to commercially available satellite imagery.[[xxv]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef325)

![](https://www.criticalthreats.org/wp-content/uploads/8th-Tactical-Air-Base-March-8-2026.gif)

![](https://www.criticalthreats.org/wp-content/uploads/4th-Artesh-Ground-Forces-Aviation-Base-March-8-2026.gif)

**The IDF has struck at least two IRGC Aerospace Force command centers in Tehran since March 6,** **likely to degrade Iranian air defense capabilities and command and control.** The IRGC Aerospace Force is the principal operator of the Iranian missile and drone arsenals.[[xxvi]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef326) Commercially available satellite imagery captured on March 6 showed that the IDF struck several warehouses in the southeastern part of an IRGC Aerospace Force air defense command center in southwestern Tehran. The IDF also struck the IRGC Aerospace Force Headquarters in Tehran on March 7.[[xxvii]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef327)

**The US-Israeli combined force continued to target Iranian ballistic missile infrastructure in order to degrade Iranian missile capabilities and ultimately destroy the Iranian ballistic missile program.** The IDF has attacked over 400 targets, including ballistic missile launchers and weapons production sites, in western and central Iran over the past day.[[xxviii]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef328) The IDF has destroyed around 75 percent of Iran's missile launchers.[[xxix]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef329) Commercially available satellite imagery shows damage to the following Iranian missile bases:

- **Shiraz South Missile Base.** Commercially available satellite imagery captured on March 7 appears to show that the combined force used ground-penetrating munitions to target underground facilities at a missile base south of Shiraz, Fars Province. This base is reportedly one of at least 25 Iranian missile bases that Iran can launch medium-range ballistic missiles from.[[xxx]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef330) Israeli strikes during the June 2025 Israel-Iran War caused “light, above-ground damage” to this base, according to the Alma Research and Education Center.[[xxxi]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef331)

![](https://www.criticalthreats.org/wp-content/uploads/Shiraz-South-Missile-Base-February-27-March-7-2026.gif)

- **Khojir Military Complex.** Commercially available satellite imagery captured on March 7 shows that the combined force destroyed several structures that were flanked by earthen berms at the Khojir Military Complex in Tehran Province. An analyst at the James Martin Center for Nonproliferation Studies assessed on March 6 that the combined force struck mixing and casting buildings at the Khojir Missile Facility.[[xxxii]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef332) The same analyst assessed on March 7 that the combined force also struck mixing and casting buildings at the Shahroud Military Complex in northeastern Iran, as CTP-ISW reported in its March 8 Morning Update.[[xxxiii]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef333) Satellite imagery published by Reuters in July 2024 showed that Iran had established 30 new buildings at the Khojir facility between August 2023 and April 2024.[[xxxiv]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef334) The IDF previously struck the Khojir Military Complex in June 2025 and October 2024.[[xxxv]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef335)

![](https://www.criticalthreats.org/wp-content/uploads/Khojir-Military-Complex-Hajarabad-March-1-3-2026.gif)

- **Chamran Missile Base.** Commercially available satellite imagery captured on March 6 shows that the combined force damaged above-ground structures at the Chamran Missile Base in Jam, Bushehr Province. Iran stores Qiam-1 ballistic missiles, which have a range of around 800 kilometers, at the Chamran Missile Base.[[xxxvi]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef336)
- **Garmdareh Missile Base.** Commercially available satellite imagery from March 6 indicates that the combined force struck above-ground and underground structures at the Garmdareh Missile Base in Alborz Province. The IDF destroyed a building at an IRGC missile integration facility during the Israel-Iran War that appears to be part of the Garmdareh Missile Base.[[xxxvii]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef337)
- **Shiraz West Missile Base.** Commercially available satellite imagery from March 7 indicates that the combined force struck above-ground and underground structures at a missile base west of Shiraz, Fars Province. The combined force has struck the Shiraz West Missile Base at least twice during the war.[[xxxviii]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef338)
- **Khorgu Missile Base.** Commercially available satellite imagery from March 7 shows that the combined force damaged several structures at the Khorgu Missile Base north of Bandar Abbas, Hormozgan Province. It is unclear if the damage in the new satellite imagery is from recent airstrikes or airstrikes that the combined force previously conducted against the base around March 1.[[xxxix]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef339)

Satellite imagery captured on March 7 also confirms that the IDF struck the Shahroud and Parchin military complexes in Semnan and Tehran provinces, respectively, as CTP-ISW reported in its [March 8 Morning Update](https://understandingwar.org/research/middle-east/iran-update-morning-special-report-march-8-2026/).[[xl]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef340)

![](https://www.criticalthreats.org/wp-content/uploads/US-Israeli-Strikes-to-Degrade-the-Iranian-Ballistic-Missile-Program-from-February-28-2026-to-March-8-2026.png)

**The combined force continued to target Iranian internal security institutions in Tehran City and western Iran.** The IDF announced on March 8 that the IAF conducted airstrikes targeting an IRGC Ground Forces compound, an unspecified “internal security command center,” an unspecified Basij base, and 50 Law Enforcement Command (LEC) ammunition bunkers in Tehran.[[xli]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef341) Two open-source intelligence (OSINT) accounts reported and geolocated combined force airstrikes on a Basij base near Yas Boulevard in southeastern Tehran City.[[xlii]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef342)

The combined force reportedly struck several internal security sites in western Iran. An OSINT analyst and anti-regime media reported that the combined force struck an IRGC headquarters and an LEC police station in Khorramabad, Lorestan Province, on March 8.[[xliii]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef343) OSINT accounts also reported combined force airstrikes targeting LEC headquarters in Borujerd, Lorestan Province, Kuhdasht, Lorestan Province, Eslamabad-e Gharb, Kermanshah Province, and Abdanan, Ilam Province.[[xliv]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef344) CTP-ISW observed protests in Eslamabad-e Gharb, Borujerd, and Abdanan during the December 2025-January 2026 protests.[[xlv]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef345)

![](https://www.criticalthreats.org/wp-content/uploads/US-and-Israeli-Strikes-Against-Law-Enforcement-Command-Headquarters-Evening-of-March-8-2026.png)

**The combined force also struck internal security institutions in southwestern and central Iran on March 8.** Anti-regime media reported that the combined force airstrike targeted the Artesh Ground Forces 92nd Division in Ahvaz, Khuzestan Province, on March 8**.**[[xlvi]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef346) The Artesh has deployed its ground forces to support protest suppression in previous protest movements.[[xlvii]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef347) OSINT accounts reported that the combined force also targeted several internal security institutions in Esfahan Province, including an LEC headquarters in Shahin Shahr on March 8.[[xlviii]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef348)

**OSINT analysts reported that the combined force struck a fuel tank at Qeshm International Airport on Qeshm Island in the Persian Gulf on March 8.[[xlix]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef349)**

**The IDF confirmed that it killed newly appointed Supreme Leader Office Military Chief and Khatam ol Anbia Central Headquarters Chief of Staff Brigadier General Abu al Qassem Babaeiyan on March 7.[[l]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef350)** Babaeiyan replaced former Supreme Leader Office Military Chief Mohammad Shirazi after the combined force killed Shirazi on February 28.[[li]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef351) The IDF reported that Babaeiyan was appointed as the Khatam on Anbia Central Headquarters Chief of Staff after the IDF killed his predecessor, Brigadier General Ali Shadmani, during the June 2025 Israel-Iran War.[[lii]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef352)

![](https://www.criticalthreats.org/wp-content/uploads/US-and-Israeli-Strikes-in-Iran-from-March-7-2026-at-3-PM-ET-to-March-8-2026-at-3-PM-ET.png)

![](https://www.criticalthreats.org/wp-content/uploads/US-and-Israeli-Strikes-in-Iran-from-February-28-2026-to-March-8-2026-GIF.gif)

**The combined force is reportedly considering deploying special forces at a later time in the war to move Iran’s highly enriched uranium (HEU) stockpile out of the country.** Four sources with knowledge of the matter told Axios on March 8 that the US administration has discussed a potential future operation to move Iran’s HEU out of Iran or dilute it in Iran.[[liii]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef353) The sources added that the combined force would only conduct such an operation if Iran could no longer seriously threaten US or Israeli troops.[[liv]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef354) US Secretary of State Marco Rubio said during a congressional briefing on March 3 that “people are going to have to go and get it” in response to a question about whether Iran’s HEU would be secured.[[lv]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef355) Iran’s HEU is buried underneath rubble at the Esfahan, Fordow, and Natanz nuclear sites, which the United States and Israel struck during the 12-day war.[[lvi]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef356) US and Israeli officials told Axios that most of Iran’s HEU stockpile is located in the underground tunnels of the nuclear facility in Esfahan, while the rest is split between Fordow and Natanz.[[lvii]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef357) An Israeli analyst, citing March 2 satellite imagery, reported that the combined force struck Natanz and severely damaged at least three buildings.[[lviii]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef358)

**Iranian Internal Security**

**The IRGC Intelligence Organization announced on March 8 that security forces arrested a 50-member cell “affiliated with a royalist group,” likely in reference to the Mojahedin-e Khalq (MEK), in Kohgiluyeh and Boyer Ahmad Province.[[lix]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef359)** Security forces reportedly seized the cell's firearms and knives.[[lx]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef360) The IRGC Intelligence Organization claimed that the group planned to conduct “sabotage” operations.[[lxi]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef361) The MEK is an Iranian opposition group.[[lxii]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef362)

**The LEC Border Guard announced on March 8 that its forces arrested a “terrorist” and seized 20 weapons in an unspecified location in Sistan and Baluchistan Province, southeastern Iran.[[lxiii]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef363)** The LEC Border Guard claimed that the “terrorist” confessed to being a leader in the December 2025-January 2026 protests.[[lxiv]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef364)

**Iran continues to face fuel shortages.** The Tehran Province Governor announced on March 8 that the maximum volume of gasoline that Tehran residents can purchase has decreased from 30 liters to 20 liters.[[lxv]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef365) The Petroleum Products Distribution CEO also called on Iranians not to panic buy fuel following Israeli airstrikes targeting oil refineries and depots in Tehran City.[[lxvi]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef366)

**Iranian Retaliation**

**Iran launched eight missile barrages at Israel between 3:00 PM ET on March 7 and 3:00 PM ET on March 8.[[lxvii]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef367)** Israel’s national emergency service, Magen David Adom, reported on March 8 that an Iranian cluster munition wounded at least six individuals in central Israel.[[lxviii]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef368) Iran has launched at least six missile barrages at Israel per day since March 2.[[lxix]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef369)

**Iran continued to attack regional countries on March 7 and 8.** An Iranian drone damaged a desalination plant in Bahrain.[[lxx]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef370) Bahrain’s water and electricity authority stated that the strike has not affected the country’s water supply. Iran also launched two drones targeting the Shaybah Oil Field in Saudi Arabia, but Saudi air defenses intercepted the drones.[[lxxi]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef371) The Saudi Civil Defense Authority reported on March 8 that an unspecified projectile fell on a residential area in Kharj, Saudi Arabia, killing two individuals and wounding 12 others.[[lxxii]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef372) The IRGC announced on March 8 that it had targeted radar systems in Kharj.[[lxxiii]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef373)

**Saudi Arabia reportedly warned Iran on March 5 that it may retaliate against Iran if Iran continues to attack Saudi territory and critical energy facilities.**[[lxxiv]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef374) Four sources told Reuters on March 7 that Saudi Foreign Minister Faisal bin Farhan told Iranian Foreign Minister Abbas Araghchi on March 5 that the Saudi government would permit US forces to use Saudi bases for military operations against Iran if Iran continues to attack critical energy facilities and Saudi territory.[[lxxv]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef375) Farhan conveyed this warning before Iranian President Masoud Pezeshkian’s March 7 apology to regional countries for Iran’s attacks on them since the start of the war.[[lxxvi]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef376) Iran has continued to conduct drone and ballistic missile attacks targeting Gulf states despite Pezeshkian’s order to suspend strikes on Gulf states unless attacks on Iran originate from their territory.[[lxxvii]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef377)

**Axis Response**

**Hezbollah has conducted two attacks targeting IDF forces and positions in northern Israel since CTP-ISW's last data cutoff at 8:00 AM ET on March 8.** Hezbollah claimed that it conducted 7 attacks as of 3:00 PM ET on March 8, which is significantly fewer attacks than Hezbollah’s 33 claimed attacks on March 7.[[lxxviii]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef378) Hezbollah claimed that it conducted two rocket attacks targeting the IDF Misgav Base and the town of Kiryat Shmona in northern Israel.[[lxxix]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef379) The IDF intercepted three drones launched from Lebanon on March 8.[[lxxx]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef380) The IDF reported that Hezbollah has been launching most of its attacks from positions deep inside southern Lebanon and not from positions along the Israel-Lebanon border.[[lxxxi]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef381) Hezbollah claimed that it attacked the IDF Rehavam Camp in Ramla, which is approximately 135 kilometers from the Israel-Lebanon border, on March 7, as CTP-ISW reported in its [March 8 Morning Update](https://understandingwar.org/research/middle-east/iran-update-morning-special-report-march-8-2026/).[[lxxxii]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef382) The IDF’s statement regarding Hezbollah’s firing position indicates that Hezbollah likely used its medium- or long-range rockets or missiles, such as the Khaibar-1 rocket, Fadi 6 rocket, or Fateh-110 ballistic missile, in this attack.[[lxxxiii]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef383) CTP-ISW previously forecasted in a [February 28 report](https://understandingwar.org/research/middle-east/hezbollahs-limited-options-for-supporting-iran/) that Hezbollah would likely participate in the war and use its long-range weapons to conduct attacks against Israel from central and northern Lebanon.[[lxxxiv]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef384)

**The IDF reported that Hezbollah attacked an Israeli D9 armored bulldozer in southern Lebanon, killing two IDF soldiers.[[lxxxv]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef385) This attack marks the first time that Hezbollah has killed an IDF soldier since the Fall 2024 Israel-Hezbollah conflict.[[lxxxvi]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef386)**

**The IDF has continued to conduct airstrikes targeting Hezbollah across Lebanon to degrade Hezbollah’s ability to launch attacks targeting Israeli forces and positions in northern Israel and southern Lebanon.** The IDF has conducted over 100 strikes in Lebanon in the past 24 hours and a total of 600 strikes using 820 munitions in Lebanon since the war began on February 28.[[lxxxvii]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef387) The IDF targeted weapons depots, dozens of military sites, a Radwan Force headquarters, a Radwan Force training compound, rocket launchers, and fighters on March 7 and 8.[[lxxxviii]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef388) The IDF confirmed that it killed the following IRGC Quds Force officers in Beirut:

- **Majid Hassani.[[lxxxix]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef389)** Hassani was an IRGC Quds Force commander responsible for transferring Iranian funds to Iranian officials in Lebanon to finance Hezbollah, the IRGC Quds Force’s Lebanon Corps and Palestine Corps, and Hamas. Hassani was also responsible for purchasing and procuring weapons for Hezbollah.
- **Ali Reza Beyaz.[[xc]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef390)** Beyaz was the IRGC Quds Force’s Lebanon Corps intelligence branch commander. The IDF stated that Beyaz was a “key knowledge center” and gathered intelligence on behalf of Hezbollah.
- **Ahmad Rasouli.[[xci]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef391)** Rasouli was an intelligence officer in the IRGC Quds Force’s Palestine Corps. Rasouli was responsible for collecting intelligence for Palestinian militias in Lebanon and the Gaza Strip.
- **Hossein Ahmadlou.[[xcii]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef392)** Ahmadlou was an intelligence operative who gathered intelligence on Israel. It is unclear if Ahmadlou was a member of Hezbollah or the IRGC Quds Force.
- **Abu Mohammad Ali.[[xciii]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef393)** Ali was Hezbollah’s representative in the IRGC Quds Force’s Palestine Corps. Ali was responsible for coordinating between Hezbollah and the Palestine Corps.

**The IDF has continued to conduct “forward defense maneuver[s]” in southern Lebanon.[[xciv]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef394)** The IDF 769th Regional Brigade (91st Division) identified a Hezbollah cell in southern Lebanon and directed an airstrike against the cell “during the past week.”[[xcv]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef395) 769th Regional Brigade forces also located a weapons stockpile in southern Lebanon that included explosive devices, light weapons, wireless devices, and ammunition. The IDF issued another evacuation warning for all residents south of the Litani River on March 8.[[xcvi]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef396)

**Iranian-backed Iraqi militias continue to claim attacks against US forces.** The Islamic Resistance in Iraq, which is a coalition of Iranian-backed Iraqi militias, claimed on March 7 that it conducted 24 drone and missile attacks targeting "enemy" bases in Iraq and the region in the past 24 hours.[[xcvii]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef397) CTP-ISW has not observed any indications of Iraqi militias using missiles in their attacks as of this writing. Likely Iranian-backed Iraqi militia front group Rijal al Baas al Shadid claimed that it conducted three drone attacks targeting the former US Victory Base at Baghdad International Airport on March 7.[[xcviii]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef398) A security source told Iraqi media on March 8 that air defenses thwarted an attempted attack on the base.[[xcix]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef399) Iranian-backed Iraqi militia Kataib Sarkhat al Quds also claimed on March 7 that it conducted a drone attack targeting Harir Airbase at the Erbil International Airport.[[c]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef3100) An unspecified Iraqi source told Iranian state media on March 8 that a drone hit a radar system at the base.[[ci]](#_ednbc431a6593939f6ef5e296341ba44ef3101)
